# Claims and revisions ledger

This ledger is the single canonical home of the series' load-bearing claims, `SW-C`, and their revision history, `SW-CR`. It was split from the [master longitudinal ledger](SW_MASTER_LONGITUDINAL_LEDGER.md) on 2026-10-14 under the [synthesis architecture](../00%20Frameworks%20and%20Methods/SW_SYNTHESIS_ARCHITECTURE.md)'s split rule. The master ledger remains the canonical home of every other record family: character state (`SW-S`), relationships (`SW-R`), chronology and knowledge (`SW-K`), world (`SW-W`), prospective questions (`SW-P`), form (`SW-F`), readiness (`SW-RD`) and unit closure (`SW-U`). The current entrypoint is [the corpus map](../CURRENT_STATE_AND_CORPUS_MAP.md). Volume readings own the source-bearing observations that claims cite.

## 1. Authority and migration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Responsibility | Current claim formulations; support, counterevidence and alternatives; boundaries, confidence and state; dependent artifacts; the append-only revision-event stream; review receipts; correction and supersession routing; claim-level audit rules. |
| Exclusive | Yes. No other file holds a live copy of any `SW-C` or `SW-CR` record. The master ledger keeps a routing statement only. |
| Origin | Section 5 of the master ledger, version 1.19, at branch commit `ee2f068`: lines 1514 to 2631, moved verbatim into section 5 below. |
| Identity preservation | 104 claims, SW-C-0001 to SW-C-0104, and 916 revision events, SW-CR-0001 to SW-CR-0916. None was renumbered, merged, deleted or given a successor. |
| Changes at migration | Fifteen lines of the moved block differ from the source. Thirteen are corrections of revision events, two are schema clarifications. All are listed in section 7 with their original text. Every other moved line is byte-identical. |
| Report | The [ledger architecture correction report](../08%20Audits%20and%20Manifests/SW_LEDGER_ARCHITECTURE_CORRECTION_REPORT.md) records the counts, checks and decisions. |

The section numbers below keep the moved block's heading, "5. Load-bearing claims and revision history", so that a reference to that section of the master ledger finds the same heading here.

## 2. Field semantics and current-view boundaries

The claim table in section 5 was written at V01 and kept its columns through sixteen units. The audit at migration found that three of its columns had stopped meaning what a reader of a current view would assume:

- **Range; class.** For claims registered at V01 to V03 the range was widened at the V04 review and then left unchanged; from V04 on it records only the registering unit. It is neither the original registration boundary nor the current evidence boundary.
- **Support.** It cites the observations available at registration. Later support is recorded in the revision events, not here.
- **State; confidence.** It opens with the state at registration or at V02, followed by an index of every later event. Its opening state can be stale: some claims still read OPEN, or "no hypothesis registered", although a later revision resolved them.

These historical fields are kept as written. Their headings are clarified in section 5 (correction CX-15), and the current view is carried by the index in section 4, with four defined boundaries:

| Boundary | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Origin boundary | The unit at whose closure the claim was registered. |
| Last formulation change | The revision event, and its unit, that last changed the wording. "None" means the registered wording stands. |
| Current evidence boundary | The latest unit whose evidence strengthened or revised the claim. Later units reviewed it without change. |
| Last reviewed boundary | The latest unit at whose closure the claim was reviewed. |

The formulation column is current. The migration checked every claim's formulation against the new formulation of its last wording-changing event, and all 104 match.

## 3. Claim review and revision events: the maintenance contract

Before migration every closed unit generated one revision event for every existing claim, whether or not the claim changed. Of 916 events, 574 are routine PRESERVE records that repeat an unchanged formulation in full. They record that a review took place, but they make the event stream harder to read and give no new evidence. The historical stream is kept in full. From the next admitted unit on, the following contract applies.

**A. Claim review.** At each unit's closure the integrator reviews every applicable existing claim against the unit's evidence. A review has one of five results:

1. no material bearing;
2. relevant evidence, no change;
3. new corroboration;
4. new counterevidence;
5. a material change of wording, scope, mechanism or confidence.

**B. Review receipt.** The unit's closure record (`SW-U` in the master ledger) and the reading's section 9.1 record the review: the claim set reviewed, the review boundary, and the exceptions, meaning every claim with a result from 3 to 5 or a significant result 2. Section 6 below gives the receipt for each unit already closed.

**C. Revision events.** A revision event, `SW-CR`, is written only for:

- **STRENGTHEN**, when new evidence materially strengthens the warrant or extends the evidential basis. Another appearance of the same kind of evidence is not enough. The event cites the new observations.
- **REVISE**, **DOWNGRADE**, **REJECT** and **OPEN**, with their defined meanings in section 5.
- **PRESERVE**, only when a serious alternative, an apparent contradiction, a test of a prospective question, or material new evidence makes an explicit decision to keep the claim significant. The event states that reason and cites the evidence.
- **Correction**, when an error is found. The event or correction entry keeps the earlier and the revised text, and it is listed in section 7.

A routine review with result 1 or 2 writes no event. It is recorded in the receipt.

**D. Current view.** Any event that changes wording, support, state or boundary also updates the claim's row in section 5 and its line in the section 4 index. The index is derived from the event stream and the claim rows. If they disagree, the event stream governs and the index is corrected.

**E. Historical reconstruction.** A claim's state at any earlier boundary is the registered formulation plus the events up to that boundary. The rule change does not alter this, because a routine review never changed a formulation.

## 4. Current view index

Derived at migration from the claim rows and the event stream, as of the last review at ANOTHER-LOWER. Events are given as PRESERVE/STRENGTHEN/REVISE counts, with partial and compound forms counted under the stronger transition. The state column is the current state. Where the claim row's opening state differs, the note gives the revision event that changed it. This index introduces no new adjudication. Where a recorded open component is not carried by the current wording, the note says so instead of resolving it.

## 5. Load-bearing claims and revision history — SW-C / SW-CR

SW-C owns the current bounded formulation and its retrieval route. Fields: claim ID; question/domain; formulation; source/knowledge range; class; support; counterevidence; alternatives; confidence and reason; current transition/state; discriminating observation; dependent artifacts/IDs; revision references.

SW-CR owns append-only change events. Fields: revision ID; claim ID; earlier formulation and boundary; transition; new formulation and boundary; new support/counterevidence; reason; affected homes; correction versus later reinterpretation; date/reviewer. Allowed transitions:

| Transition | Meaning |
|---|---|
| PRESERVE | Material formulation retained after review. |
| STRENGTHEN | Same formulation receives stronger independent support. |
| REVISE | Wording, scope or mechanism materially changes. |
| DOWNGRADE | Confidence or warranted scope decreases. |
| REJECT | Claim is contradicted sufficiently to cease being accepted. |
| OPEN | Discriminating evidence remains insufficient or conflicting. |

An initial claim has no invented predecessor. It may begin OPEN or another justified initial assessment; a revision event requires an actual previous formulation. Preserve rejected claims for provenance rather than deleting their IDs. Normative criteria are explicit and separate from evidence confidence.

Claim records. Rows SW-C-0001 to SW-C-0018 were registered at V01, rows SW-C-0019 to SW-C-0026 at V02, rows SW-C-0027 to SW-C-0034 at V03, and rows SW-C-0035 onward at V04. Every row shows the current formulation after V04 review; earlier wording is preserved in the revision records below. *Corrected at migration, 2026-10-14 (CX-14, CX-15):* rows SW-C-0035 to SW-C-0040 were registered at V04, SW-C-0041 to 0044 at AFTER04, 0045 to 0050 at V05, 0051 to 0056 at V06, 0057 to 0062 at V07, 0063 to 0068 at V08, 0069 to 0074 at V09, 0075 to 0078 at EXTRA09, 0079 to 0084 at V10, 0085 to 0089 at V11, 0090 to 0094 at V12, 0095 to 0099 at ANOTHER-UPPER and 0100 to 0104 at ANOTHER-LOWER. Every row shows the current formulation after the last review, at ANOTHER-LOWER. The third, fifth and seventh columns are historical fields: their current meaning is given in section 2, and the [current-view index](#4-current-view-index) gives each claim's current boundaries and state.

| SW-C-0043 | Effort against strength | Cyril’s ethic of earned, patient effort is set against Louis’s maxim of money, violence and strength; Glen and Monica, who drifted into magic, take Cyril’s side. At V11 Louis names his adult method as money, violence and connections, learned from his defeat by Monica at the council, and sets what he has gained against Adolf’s reliance on borrowed power. The prequel shows the maxim’s holder as himself a genius of effort driven by refusal to lose, so that the opposition lies in motive and display rather than in effort. | AFTER04; SI | AFTER04 obs. 075, 079, 090, 099 | Louis’s maxim as joke only. | Recorded. | SW-CR-0119 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V05) | See V05 reading section 9.1. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0120 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she also uses it deliberately to compute combat and to bear people, and it fails when people she knows are threatened. (prior) | REVISE | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (V05) | Numbers as evidence (V05 obs. 139, 144). | See V05 reading section 9.1. | Revision on new evidence. |
| SW-CR-0121 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V05) | See V05 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

| SW-CR-0163 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V06) | See V06 reading section 9.1. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0164 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V06) | See V06 reading section 9.1. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0165 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V06) | See V06 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

(prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V06) | See V06 reading section 9.1. | — | Strengthened. |

(prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V06) | See V06 reading section 9.1. | — | Strengthened. |

(prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V06) | See V06 reading section 9.1. | — | Strengthened. |

| SW-CR-0269 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V08) | See V08 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0270 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V08) | See V08 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0271 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V08) | See V08 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0272 | SW-C-0004 | The makeweight self-assessment is contradicted by Louis’s memory of the duel; the examiners’ ranking is unstated. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V08) | See V08 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0273 | SW-C-0005 | Stated protection; Monica doubts it; Louis reads surveillance, uses a decoy, chose Monica because she distrusts, fears and hates people and can therefore be merciless, and calls himself first-prince because he dislikes the duke and Felix; the king is unshown. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V08) | See V08 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

| SW-CR-0275 | SW-C-0007 | Public and private selves diverge: he denies a talent for magic while studying it in secret because it is forbidden to him, and hides a spirit; he obeys the duke outwardly while removing his placements and gathering nobles who resent him; he must become king without wanting it, under a ten-year vow to make the name Felix Arc Ridill remembered, which he now expresses as a hero’s posthumous light and pursues even against his dead friend’s wish. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V08) | V08 obs. 153, 160: magic forbidden because affinities differ. | — | Strengthened. |

| SW-CR-0331 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V09) | See V09 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0332 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V09) | See V09 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0333 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V09) | See V09 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0334 | SW-C-0004 | The makeweight self-assessment is contradicted by Louis’s memory of the duel; the examiners’ ranking is unstated. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V09) | See V09 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

(prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V09) | See V09 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

| SW-CR-0399 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (EXTRA09) | EXTRA09 obs. 003: numbers not frightening. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0400 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (EXTRA09) | EXTRA09 obs. 003, 028: refuge in numbers. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0401 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. | SW-CR-0473 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V10) | See V10 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0474 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V10) | V10 obs. 057: faces remembered by ratios. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0475 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V10) | See V10 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

Revision records at V11, 2026-10-14, reviewer the V11 sequential integrator:

| Revision | Claim | Earlier formulation and boundary | Transition | New formulation and boundary | Reason | Affected homes | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SW-CR-0551 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0552 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0553 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0554 | SW-C-0004 | The makeweight self-assessment is contradicted by Louis’s memory of the duel; the examiners’ ranking is unstated. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0555 | SW-C-0005 | Stated protection; Monica doubts it; Louis reads surveillance, uses a decoy, chose Monica because she distrusts, fears and hates people and can therefore be merciless, and calls himself first-prince because he dislikes the duke and Felix; the king is unshown. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0556 | SW-C-0006 | The prologue’s watcher is Felix. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0557 | SW-C-0007 | Public and private selves diverge: he denies a talent for magic while studying it in secret because it is forbidden to him, and hides a spirit; he obeys the duke outwardly while removing his placements and gathering nobles who resent him; he must become king without wanting it, under a ten-year vow to make the name Felix Arc Ridill remembered, which he now expresses as a hero’s posthumous light and pursues even against his dead friend’s wish. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V11) | See V11 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

| Revision | Claim | Earlier formulation and boundary | Transition | New formulation and boundary | Reason | Affected homes | Kind |

| SW-CR-0635 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V12) | See V12 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0636 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V12) | V12 obs. 054: fear of tall men from her uncle. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0637 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | STRENGTHEN | Wording unchanged; support extended (V12) | V12 obs. 054: the uncle who beat her. | — | Strengthened. |
| SW-CR-0638 | SW-C-0004 | The makeweight self-assessment is contradicted by Louis’s memory of the duel; the examiners’ ranking is unstated. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (V12) | See V12 reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

Revision records at ANOTHER-UPPER, 2026-10-14, reviewer the -another- (upper) sequential integrator:

| Revision | Claim | Earlier formulation and boundary | Transition | New formulation and boundary | Reason | Affected homes | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SW-CR-0724 | SW-C-0001 | Chantless casting began as avoidance of speech, and silence and mastery are one mechanism; the avoidance has an earlier root in childhood punishment for speaking; before Minerva a year of abuse produced a dissociation in which she lost language, ended by Hilda Everett’s rescue; its occasion at Minerva was a friend’s plan to shorten her chant; the public account explains the capacity as instant computation. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (ANOTHER-UPPER) | See -another- (upper) reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0725 | SW-C-0002 | Pain, fear or exposure drive Monica into numerical absorption; the habit began as a child’s escape from her uncle into her father’s formulas and a vow to keep his numbers; she uses it to compute combat and to bear people, it fails when people she knows are threatened, and she now turns it outward as evidence, naming it no longer a refuge. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (ANOTHER-UPPER) | See -another- (upper) reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0726 | SW-C-0003 | Monica’s paternal uncle, after her father’s execution when she was ten, beat and starved her, shut her in a winter barn and forbade her to defend her father, for about a year until Hilda Everett took her; scars remain and touch still frightens her. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (ANOTHER-UPPER) | See -another- (upper) reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |
| SW-CR-0727 | SW-C-0004 | The makeweight self-assessment is contradicted by Louis’s memory of the duel; the examiners’ ranking is unstated. (prior) | PRESERVE | Unchanged (ANOTHER-UPPER) | See -another- (upper) reading section 9.1. | — | Review, not change. |

## 8. Write, amend and audit rules

- Append new revision events under a "Revision records at <unit>" heading, in ID order. Never renumber, merge or delete an event or a claim. A rejected claim stays in place with its REJECT event.
- Correct an objective error through a correction entry in section 7, keeping the original text, and mark the corrected field with its correction ID.
- After each unit, verify mechanically that every claim row's formulation equals the new formulation of its last wording-changing event, that every event names an existing claim, and that the section 4 index agrees with the stream.
- Dependent artifacts cite claims by stable ID. A revision that changes a claim's meaning lists the affected homes in its event.
- Split this ledger further only if current claims and the historical stream acquire independent revision pressure. Size alone is not a reason.
